D3 records what lives upstream (`frames_<RULE>`, `merge_<RULE>`, no thread dependency) and what is Nikaia's driver. D5 records that bounded repetition and the single-digit `digit` terminal now exist.
- **Spec Part II (10.7)**: the three-class check, the lexical requirement, `MEASUREMENT` uppercase.
- **Examples**: `1brc.nika` — `MEASUREMENT` is lexical, `TENTHS` uses `digit{1,2}` and the `DIGITS`/`DIGIT` helper rules are gone, `NAME`'s comment explains the bounding; `examples/README.md` moves G9 to Resolved and updates G4 with what the backend now provides.
- **Deps**: `winnow-grammar` advanced from `f4955bc` to current `main` — bounded repetition, the `digit` terminal, and the word-at-a-time scan behind `until`/`recover` (which now yields the skipped text).

### Added (0.0.8 - Input Provenance)
- **Spec/ADR**: ADR-010 "Input Provenance — Where the Security Requirement Is Stated" — resolves gap **G8** and **corrects ADR-009 §5's framing**, which posed the hasher question as a profile problem. The profile answers "which runtime", never "who supplied these bytes": a single-threaded Lite server hashing attacker-supplied header names is exactly as vulnerable as an Advanced one, and a compute job over operator-chosen data has no adversary in either. Decisions D1-D8: trust is a two-state lattice (`Trusted ⊑ Untrusted`) on *sources*, travelling the provenance edges ADR-008 already tracks, recorded in the Borrow Contract Ledger, solved by the same monotone fixpoint (so ADR-005 D8's determinism covers it unchanged) and **failing safe** at `dyn`/FFI barriers — the opposite direction from ADR-008 D7's representation barrier, deliberately; `std` classifies its own sources by channel (network, IPC and database rows untrusted — stored data is attacker-chosen data with a delay; files, argv, env and compile-time data trusted); the user has the last word at the place data enters, in both directions (`fs::map(path; trusted: false)`), with every assertion recorded in one auditable file; a grammar may pin an `@untrusted` floor its callers cannot lower, which is the DSL's answer, while no `@trusted` floor exists because a format cannot make its input safe; the compiler then picks only the hasher — keyed with a per-process random seed for untrusted keys, fast for trusted ones, with the build failing rather than falling back to a fixed seed where there is no entropy source — and never weakens equality or layout; map iteration order is consequently unspecified and may vary between runs; `nikaia explain --trust` makes every choice visible. D8 keeps the mechanism general (G7's HTML escaping is the obvious second consumer) while recording that escaping at a template hole must stay unconditional: provenance may add lints there, never switch escaping off.

### Changed (0.0.8)
- **Spec Part I (4.5)**: the map introduction notes that hashing follows key provenance and that iteration order is not guaranteed.
- **Spec Part III (17.1)**: new `std::collections` section — the attack, the two provenances, the inheritance and join rules, the user override at the source, the grammar floor, and `nikaia explain --trust`.
- **Spec/ADR-009**: §5's open question on the hasher is replaced by a pointer to ADR-010, including the record that its own framing was wrong.
- **Examples**: `examples/README.md` moves G8 to Resolved; G5 (ordered iteration) notes that random seeding makes the explicit ordered form the only correct answer, and G7 records ADR-010 D8's boundary condition.

### Added (0.0.8 - Parallel Parsing)
- **Spec/ADR**: ADR-009 "Parallel Parsing — Frames, Monoid Folds, and Format Assumptions in the Grammar" — closes gap **G4** by dissolving it. Decisions D1-D7: `@frame` marks a rule as a resynchronization unit and the compiler **verifies** the property (the boundary must not be consumable inside a frame, so CSV with quoted newlines is rejected instead of silently miscounted) rather than trusting an assertion; `par_fold(rule, init, step, merge)` supplies the second half, and parallel parsing is exactly a frame plus a monoid; the lowering (blind split, seam repair, frame ownership, EOF and oversized-frame edge cases, absolute error offsets) is normative; byte scanning is a **specified complexity** — never one byte at a time — with portable word-at-a-time SWAR as the baseline and SIMD only as a target-gated layer, so Lite and `wasm32` keep the same performance story; fixed-width numeric parsing requires bounded repetition in the grammar rather than compiler guesswork; a view may be hashed from its first bytes but equality stays full-content; a large read-only mapping may be left to the OS at process exit. Parallelism is opt-in: a plain `fold` is never parallelised behind the user's back, because a merge over floats would make the result depend on the core count. The frame marker is **not** spelled `sync` — that keyword already means "pure CPU, cannot pause" (12.1) and names `recover`'s resynchronization token.
- **Spec Part II (10.7)**: new section "Parallel Parsing" — `@frame`, its verification, `par_fold`, why parallelism must be asked for, and the Lite degradation to a sequential fold.

### Changed (0.0.8)
- **Spec Part III (17.1)**: `fs::map` notes that a read-only mapping is left to the OS at process exit (ADR-009 D7).
- **Examples**: `1brc.nika` marks `measurement` `@frame` and folds with `par_fold`; `main` loses `split_aligned`, the `par_iter`/`collect` and the merge loop, and is now `let totals = dsl Measurements from data`. `examples/README.md` moves G4 to Resolved and records two new open gaps: **G8** (the default hasher — a DoS-resistance-versus-throughput decision that cannot be made per profile without reopening ADR-005 D8c, deferred to its own ADR) and **G9** (bounded repetition `p{n,m}`, an upstream `winnow-grammar` request; the pinned `f4955bc` has `*`, `+`, `?`, `count`, `fold` and no `{n,m}`).

### Added (0.0.8 - Tethered Slices in User Structs)
- **Spec/ADR**: ADR-008 "Tethered Slices in User Structs (the Tether-State Lattice)" — closes gap **G3** and refines ADR-005 D4. Decisions D1-D9: view types stay (`&str` in a field is a view marker, not a lifetime — options "one `String` type with silent copies" and "one `String` type with a hard error" both rejected as the default rule); a three-state lattice `Borrowed ⊑ Tethered ⊑ Owned` solved **per construction site**; uniform state per struct instance, so a struct has at most three layouts and monomorphization is bounded by the lattice height; the `Shared` handle sits on the **container**, not on each slice; promotion to `Owned` is never automatic, and the residual "buffer cannot be shared" case is a hard error with a fix-it; `@borrowed` as an opt-in compile-time assertion for hot structs; the state joins the Borrow Contract Ledger and inherits ADR-005 D8's determinism, with `dyn`/API boundaries as state barriers that widen to Tethered (never to Owned); buffer retention as a named lint rather than a silent leak; explicit region binding rejected as the core mechanism.

### Changed (0.0.8)
- **Spec Part I (6.6)**: the rule is restated over the right event — "transient = borrow, **escaping** = tether, copying = yours to ask for" instead of "stored = tether". As written before, every struct holding a slice took a shared handle, which in `1brc.nika` means a `Shared` handle on a struct built ~10^9 times and, under the Advanced profile, a billion atomic increment/decrement pairs on one refcount word contended by every worker. Storing a slice in a struct is not by itself an escape. The section gains the state table, the container-level handle rule, `@borrowed`, `nikaia explain --tethers`, and the honest cost (a tether pins the whole buffer).
- **Spec Part II (10.6)**: zero-copy parsing follows the corrected rule; the handle is per container (one for an AST), not per token, and tokens that never escape cost nothing.
- **Spec Part III (17.1)**: `fs::map` documents retention — a small extract escaping a large map pins the whole map, warned about with a `.to_owned()` fix-it, and the mapping may be released later than the block that created it.
- **Examples**: `1brc.nika` marks `Reading` `@borrowed` and explains why both the struct field and the `HashMap` key stay plain references; `examples/README.md` moves G3 to Resolved. G4 (line-aligned chunking) remains open.

### Added (0.0.7 - Grammar Protocol)
- **Spec/ADR**: ADR-007 "Scannerless Grammar Protocol & Hybrid DSL Binding" — recovers and re-decides RFC 0.0.6, whose spec text was reverted on `main` by `c6ff1fa`. Decisions D1-D7: scannerless parsing as a language guarantee; `} eod` explicit DSL termination enabling parallel parsing of the rest of the file; `dsl` as an expression unifying compile-time and runtime parsing; hybrid binding (`meta::capture` vs. `meta::parameter`); shadow types with the typed spread `...args: Self::dsl`; hardware instructions moved out of the core; auto-AST and regex terminals explicitly deferred. Records what `winnow-grammar` already validates (cut operator `=>`, `recover`, scannerless input) and the rejected alternatives.
- **Spec Part II (10.1)**: Grammars respecified as scannerless — commit points (`=>`), lexical (UPPERCASE) vs. syntactic (lowercase) rules, typed actions; auto-AST marked as not-yet-available.
- **Spec Part II (10.2)**: Dual-mode parsing now uses one syntax for both modes (`dsl G from …` in `const` vs. `let`), replacing `from … with …` and the generated `P::parse()`.
- **Spec Part II (10.5)**: The DSL protocol — `} eod`, scannerless delegation, hybrid binding table, Subject `;` Config for DSL parameters, worked SQL example with the typed spread. Names the immediate/deferred split as a recurring language principle (lambda capture 5.4, DSL binding 10.5, cleanup ADR-006 D3).
- **Spec Part III (16)**: Chapter rewritten as "Hardware Instructions (via DSL)" — `unsafe asm` and its register-constraint table removed from the core because WASM is a stack machine with no registers to constrain; `dsl backend::x86` / `dsl backend::wasm` instead; `unsafe` kept reserved for future FFI.

### Changed (0.0.7)
- **Spec Part II (10.6)**: Zero-copy parsing now specifies *both* mechanisms with a rule for choosing — interned `Symbol`s for identifiers and keywords (cheap equality, dedup), tethered slices for bulk text (string literals, comments, DSL bodies). Adds an explicit correction: drafts up to 0.0.6 claimed the borrow checker *ensures* stored tokens stay valid; it would reject them, which is what tethering exists to solve.
- **Spec Part II (10.7)**: Fault tolerance respecified on `recover(rule, sync)` with a commit point, matching the parser backend.
- **Spec Part III (15, 17)**: `dsl js` uses a parameter hole (`:msg` + `script.exec(; msg: …)`) instead of `{message}` string interpolation; `dsl sql` block terminated with `} eod`.
- **Spec/ADR-001**: Corrected — the parser is generated by `winnow-grammar` (a separate repository, pinned by git revision), not `syn-grammar`. Records why `syn-grammar` was the wrong tool (it parses a Rust `TokenStream`, so it inherits Rust's lexer and cannot host foreign DSL syntax), the backend features this architecture depends on, what the backend does *not* provide, and the version-pinning constraint.
- **Spec Part I (5.4) / Part II (11.2)**: Closed two code fences inherited from 0.0.5 that swallowed the following prose into a code block.
- **Spec Parts I-III / README**: Version bumped to 0.0.7; README roadmap now lists 0.0.5, 0.0.6 and 0.0.7 separately.

### Fixed (Toolchain / CI)
- **Build**: The `nikaia` binary failed to link (`cannot satisfy dependencies so 'std' only shows up once`, 19 errors) because `rustc-executor` pulls in the compiler's dylibs while the binary linked the ordinary rlib `std`. The binary now declares `#![feature(rustc_private)]` + `extern crate rustc_driver`, and a build script adds the toolchain's lib directory to the rpath so the linked binary starts.
- **Deps**: `winnow-grammar` advanced from `43aae1f3` to `b03a1f0f` and `winnow` from 0.6 to 0.7. Adapted to the new API: `parse_<rule>()` is a parser factory driven with `.parse_next()`, and it runs on a `Stateful` stream carrying `ParseContext`. Parse errors are now `winnow_grammar::ParseError` and are rendered with line/column and rule stack.
- **Parser**: identifiers bind the non-interning `raw_ident` terminal and the AST stores `String`; `syn::Ident`/`proc-macro2` are gone from the front-end (they were left over from the `syn-grammar` era and cannot be built from the new terminals anyway). Interning waits on an upstream `Symbol` off-by-one — see ADR-007, D7.
- **Grammar**: restored two rules the `crates/` restructure dropped although the changelog claimed them — `spawn_expr` (so `spawn({ … })` becomes `Expr::Spawn` instead of a generic call) and `block` in expression position.
- **Tests**: `tests/hello_world.rs` referenced a crate (`nikaia_driver`) that no longer exists and sat outside any package, so cargo never built it. `crates/nikaia` gained a library target and the test now runs as `crates/nikaia/tests/parser.rs`; a new `samples.rs` asserts that every `tests/samples/*.nika` parses.
- **Samples**: `let_assignment.nika` used a top-level `let`, which neither the grammar nor the spec allows; it is now a valid program.
- **Toolchain**: `rust-toolchain.toml` adds `rustfmt` and `clippy` — without rustfmt the repo's own `scripts/pre-commit.sh` could not run.
- **CI**: the workflow installed `stable` while `rust-toolchain.toml` pins a nightly, and its final step ran bare `cargo run --release` against a virtual workspace manifest, which only prints clap's usage error and exits non-zero. It now installs the pinned toolchain via `rustup show` (single source of truth), caches cargo, and runs fmt check, build, tests, and the driver over every sample.
- **Warnings**: removed unused imports across the workspace and the unread `NikaiaFrontend::backend` field; added a `trace` feature forwarding to `winnow-grammar/trace` so the `grammar!` expansion's `cfg` check is legitimate. The workspace now builds warning-free.
- **Repo**: deleted the orphaned root `build.rs` (the workspace root has no `[package]`, so cargo never ran it).

### Changed (Toolchain)
- **Deps**: `winnow-grammar` advanced to `f4955bc`, which carries two changes made upstream for this work — the `Symbol` round-trip fix (its handle was off by one through `lasso::Spur`, so `resolve` returned the neighbouring symbol's text or panicked on the last one) and the new `fold(rule, init, step)` repetition.
- **Parser**: identifiers are interned again. The grammar binds `ident`, the AST stores `winnow_grammar::Symbol`, and the interner travels with the AST as `parser::Parsed` so names can be resolved. This is Spec Part II 10.6 in practice — each distinct spelling stored once, equality an integer comparison. `syn::Ident` and `proc-macro2` stay gone. One cost worth knowing: the derived `Debug` prints `Symbol(3)` rather than the name, so `Parsed::text` is the way to read one.

### Added (Standard Library)
- **Spec Part III (17.1)**: `std::fs` now has a specified surface instead of a description. Whole-file (`read` returning a shared `Bytes` buffer so slices can be tethered to it, `read_to_string`, `write`), streaming (`lines`, `bytes` — constant memory, immediate contexts when iterated), handles (`open` → `File`, which implements `Cleanup` so a failed flush surfaces as an error rather than being swallowed, plus explicit `close()` as the opt-in for handling it at a precise point), memory mapping (`map`), metadata and directory operations, and a per-profile availability table. Every function `throws`: a missing file is an environmental error, not a bug.
- **Spec Part III (17.1)**: `fs::map`'s availability is a property of the **target**, not of the profile. Memory mapping is an operating-system service, so it works under both profiles on any target that provides it — a Lite program on Linux maps files exactly like an Advanced one. Only `wasm32-*` rules it out, where it is a compile-time error rather than a silent fallback to `read`, because degrading a map into a full read turns a constant-memory program into one that allocates its entire input. This is the opposite axis from `std::thread`, which is barred by the profile.

### Added (Examples)
- **`examples/`**: specification-level programs that the bootstrap parser cannot compile yet, kept separate from `tests/samples/` (which must parse and is covered by `cargo test`).
  - `1brc.nika` — the One Billion Row Challenge: a scannerless grammar for the **whole file** (not for one line — you write down what the input looks like and get a parser that drives itself, paying the DSL protocol once rather than a billion times), with a commit point after the separator, station names as tethered slices into a memory-mapped buffer, integer-tenths aggregation, and one parser per chunk distributed with `par_iter`. Chosen because it stresses precisely what ADR-007 asserts — if it is slow, the grammar protocol's performance argument is wrong.
  - `fortunes.nika` — the TechEmpower `fortunes` benchmark: one request handler using *both* binding modes from ADR-007 D4, deferred parameters for the SQL DSL and immediate capture for the HTML template DSL, over implicit-async I/O in the Lite profile.
  - `README.md` — survey of the benchmark suites worth targeting (1BRC, Computer Language Benchmarks Game, TechEmpower, Are We Fast Yet?), with a recommended order, plus the specification gaps writing the two programs exposed. G1 (`std::fs`) is resolved above. G2 was restated and then resolved: the original wording claimed a gap around "applying a grammar per line", which misread the language — you write down what the file looks like and get a parser. The real gap was narrower, a **folding repetition** at the entry rule; `fold` now exists in `winnow-grammar` and `1brc.nika` uses it.

### Documentation
- **Removed `docs/winnow-grammar/`**: a stale copy of another project's documentation. The one Nikaia-relevant part — the `Symbol` off-by-one that blocks interning — now sits with the decision it constrains, in ADR-007 D7.
- **Analysis**: `docs/analysis/branch-review-0.0.5-v0.0.6.md` — forensic review of the `0.0.5` and `v0.0.6` branches: both were squash-merged intact, `v0.0.6` was then reverted by `c6ff1fa`, and the resulting version-number collision. Lists the lost ideas, the convergences between the branches, and the conflicts.

### Added
- **Spec/ADR**: ADR-005 "Borrow Model & Lifetime Strategy" — normative four-group taxonomy (solved by rustc / solved by the driver / needs a language construct / genuine user bug), decisions D1–D7, rejected alternatives (implicit clone, in-source borrow annotations, struct lifetime parameters).
- **Spec Part I (6.5–6.8)**: References & Borrowing guarantees ("no lifetime annotations, ever"), Tethered Slices rule ("transient = borrow, stored = tether"), Borrow Contract Ledger overview, diagnostics promise with worked `NK2301` example.
- **Spec Part III (13.5)**: The Borrow Contract Ledger (`nikaia.contracts`) — generated committed file serving as incremental cache and as the diff basis for narrated "what changed and what broke" errors (`NK2401`).
- **Spec Part III (Appendix C)**: The Diagnostics Contract — untranslated rustc errors are compiler bugs; NK error-code catalogue with testable requirements.
- **Spec/ADR (D8)**: Determinism requirement for contract inference — the ledger is a byte-deterministic pure function of (source tree, toolchain); parallel solving allowed, cross-toolchain stability explicitly not required, one profile-neutral ledger per project, violations are compiler bugs; includes implementer ban list and CI double-build/cross-OS test definitions.
- **Spec Part III (13.5)**: Determinism guarantee and `--locked` verification mode for `nikaia.contracts`.
- **Spec/ADR**: ADR-006 "Resource Cleanup under Implicit Async" — the `Cleanup` trait (pausable `cleanup() throws` + synchronous `drop` fallback, compiler-inserted at scope exit); three-death-paths taxonomy; cleanup errors throw normally (signatures tell the truth, secondary-error attachment during unwinding, explicit `close()` opt-in); cancellation parks cleanups with the runtime; shutdown drain phase with `cleanup-deadline` and a termination argument (cannot hang, no deadlock via ADR-005 D6); rejected alternatives (blocking drop, fire-and-forget, explicit-close-only, linear types, rustc `async_drop`).
- **Spec Part I (6.4)**: `Drop` vs. `Cleanup` distinction with worked `NK2601` diagnostic, `sync`-context restriction (`NK2602`), parked-cleanup and honest Lite-panic notes.
- **Spec Part II (12.4)**: defined semantics for "cancelled and cleaned up" (parked cleanup).
- **Spec Part III**: `cleanup-deadline` manifest key (13.3); `NK26xx` resource-cleanup codes in Appendix C.
- **Spec/ADR (ADR-006 D6)**: The Panic Hook (`std::panic::on_panic`) — global per application, `sync`, runs on every panic path including Lite's abort and the WASM trap (rides on the backend invoking the hook before abort under `panic = abort`); diagnosis-not-cleanup rule, brief-blocking allowance with Advanced nuance, recursion guard; `NK2604`; stackable hook chains rejected. Spec: Part I 7.2 (worked example), 6.4 note updated, Part III Appendix A.

### Changed
- **Spec Part II (10.6)**: Zero-copy parsing respecified on Tethered Slices (`bytes::Bytes` model over `Shared`); buffer provably outlives tokens instead of borrow-checker rejection. Unsafe self-referential codegen recorded as future optimization note.
- **Spec Part II (12.2)**: "No I/O while holding a lock" is now a compile-time rule — `access`/`access_all` require a `sync` lambda in both profiles (`NK2201`); runtime reentrancy check/poisoning demoted to backstop.
- **Spec Part II (12.7)**: Scoped tasks respecified for soundness — Lite: runtime-owned scopes, any child task allowed; Advanced: child tasks must be `sync` (`NK2102`), async children use `spawn` + implicit move. Beginner-oriented rewrite with worked error messages.
- **Spec Part I (8.3)**: Resolved contradiction with 5.4/11.2 — `spawn` uses implicit move (no `move` keyword); rewritten with `.clone()` guidance and worked `NK2101` error.
- **Spec Parts I–III**: Version bumped to 0.0.6 (aligns with README badge); fixed a `nila` code-fence typo (Part I, 5.4) and an unclosed code fence (Part III, Appendix B).

### Fixed
- **Grammar**: Fixed `expr` rule to include `block`, enabling parsing of blocks in expression positions (e.g., `spawn({ ... })`).
- **Grammar**: Added specific `spawn_expr` rule to correctly parse `spawn` statements as `Expr::Spawn` instead of generic function calls.
- **Grammar**: Added `skip_ws` rule to consume trailing whitespace at the end of the program, preventing `ParseError` at EOF.
- **Grammar**: Renamed whitespace skipper rule from `ws` to `skip_ws` to avoid infinite recursion bug in `winnow-grammar`.
- **Tests**: Fixed syntax error (missing comma) in `tests/hello_world.rs`.
- **Build**: Updated `src/main.rs` and `tests/hello_world.rs` to wrap input source in `LocatingSlice` to satisfy `winnow::stream::Location` trait bounds required by the generated parser.
